<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> The limiting satisfiability threshold of uniform random 3-SAT is a computable real. We credit Gaia Carenini [[4]](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/229/) with priority for resolving the satisfiability conjecture, which establishes the threshold's existence. We prove that one finite deterministic machine can approximate it to any prescribed accuracy. A deletion estimate gives explicit lower certificates, while a finite hierarchical approximation of the soft pressure gives upper certificates at every larger rational density. A fair search through these certificates halts without requiring a computable rate of finite-size convergence.

- **claim-computable-threshold** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a real number $\alpha_3\in(0,\infty)$ such that, for every fixed real $a\ge0$, $$\lim_{n\to\infty}p(n,\lfloor an\rfloor)= \begin{cases}1,&a<\alpha_3,\\0,&a>\alpha_3.\end{cases}$$ There is one finite deterministic Turing machine which, on input $1^r$ for any integer $r\ge1$, halts with a rational number $q_r$ satisfying $\|q_r-\alpha_3\|\le2^{-r}$. Its only input is the unary precision $1^r$; it uses no oracle, advice, or noncomputable real constant.
  - Explanation: The proper random clause ensemble has a unique limiting threshold, and a fixed deterministic machine approximates that threshold from unary precision alone. The result concerns an ensemble parameter rather than assignment search.
- **claim-bilateral-certificate-search** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cert-search)): Let $\alpha\in[0,20]$. Suppose that $(\ell_k)_{k\ge1}$ is a computable sequence of rational numbers satisfying $\ell_k\le\alpha$ for every $k$ and $\ell_k\to\alpha$. Suppose also that finite descriptions $Q$ form an effectively enumerable class, and that for every positive rational $a$, positive integer $\beta$, and valid description $Q$, the real number $G(a,\beta;Q)$ is uniformly computable. Assume that* 1. *$G(a,\beta;Q)<0$ implies $\alpha\le a$;* 2. *for every rational $a>\alpha$, some positive integer $\beta$ and valid description $Q$ satisfy $G(a,\beta;Q)<0$.* *Then one finite deterministic Turing machine, with only the unary input $1^r$ for $r\ge1$, returns a rational number within $2^{-r}$ of $\alpha$. It can encode the output as a binary integer numerator and a positive integer denominator, with separators.
  - Explanation: A convergent computable lower certificate family and complete semidecidable upper witnesses suffice for a terminating approximation search. The maintained certified interval supplies the stopping rule without a convergence modulus.
- **claim-free-deletion-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-cp-deletion-moment)): For all integers $n\ge1$ and $0\le r\le n$, and every $v\in[n]$, $$\label{cp:one-delay} \mathbb E\bigl[(F_r^{+v}-F_r)^p\bigr]\le C,\qquad C=2^{60}.$$ Consequently, uniformly in $r$, $$\label{cp:concentration} \mathbb E\lvert F_r-\mathbb EF_r\rvert^p\le C_1n, \qquad C_1=2^{62}.$$
  - Explanation: The extra capped survival time from a free variable deletion has a uniform moment bound even when other deletions are allowed. Independent bucket resampling then yields concentration of the capped time.
- **claim-weighted-survival-interpolation** (bound, [source](#stmt-cp-interpolation)): For positive integers $n_1,n_2$ and real $t_1,t_2\ge0$, $$\label{cp:product-survival} P_{n_1+n_2}(t_1+t_2) \ge P_{n_1}(t_1)P_{n_2}(t_2).$$
  - Explanation: Combining independent blocks preserves at least the product of their survival probabilities at the summed arrival time. The proof first interpolates clause locations and then balances the variable weights.
- **claim-limiting-center-lower-certificate** (bound, [source](#stmt-cp-lower)): The sequence $f_n/n$ converges to a real number $\alpha$. For every integer $k\ge1$, $$\label{cp:certified-lower} \alpha\ge\frac{f_k}{k}-2^{67}k^{-1/6}.$$
  - Explanation: Normalized capped means converge, and an explicit one-sided correction converts every finite-size mean into a lower bound on the limiting center. Summable merging errors establish convergence.
- **claim-proper-clause-threshold-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cp-threshold)): The limit $\alpha$ in Proposition <a href="#cp:lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cp:lower">5</a> lies in $[1/200,10]$. For every fixed real $a\ge0$, the relaxed Poisson model satisfies $$P_n(an)\longrightarrow \begin{cases} 1,&0\le a<\alpha,\\ 0,&a>\alpha. \end{cases}$$ The same limits hold for $p(n,\lfloor an\rfloor)$ in the proper-clause model defined in the introduction. In particular its limiting density is $\alpha_3=\alpha$.
  - Explanation: The limiting capped mean is the strict-side threshold for the relaxed Poisson ensemble and the proper fixed-count ensemble. Thinning, density slack, and Poisson count concentration transfer the limits between the models.
- **claim-effective-finite-size-integration** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-cp-finite-computation)): The real $f_n$ is computable uniformly in the integer $n$. There is an effective enumeration of rational numbers strictly below $\alpha$ whose supremum is $\alpha$. In particular, there is a computable rational sequence $(\ell_n)_{n\ge1}$ with $\ell_n<\alpha$ and $\ell_n\to\alpha$.
  - Explanation: Finite clause enumeration and certified integration compute each capped expectation uniformly. Subtracting the one-sided correction and an approximation margin produces rational lower certificates approaching the threshold.
- **claim-linear-deletion-robustness** (bound, [source](#stmt-cp-robust)): For all integers $n\ge1$ and $0\le r\le n$, $$\label{cp:robust-mean} \frac{\mathbb EF_r}{n}\le\frac{f_n}{n} +C_4(r/n)^{1/6},\qquad C_4=2^{53}.$$ For every fixed real $a>\alpha$, there exists $\epsilon>0$ such that, with probability tending to one, the relaxed formula at time $an$ cannot be made satisfiable by deleting $\lfloor\epsilon n\rfloor$ variables. On the same event every assignment violates more than $\lfloor\epsilon n\rfloor$ clauses.
  - Explanation: A linear deletion allowance has a controlled effect on mean survival. Above the threshold a sufficiently small linear allowance fails with high probability, forcing a positive density of violations for every assignment.
- **claim-soft-pressure-signs** (bound, [source](#stmt-cp-soft-signs)): For every fixed real $0\le a<\alpha$ and every fixed $\beta>0$, $$\label{cp:soft-below} \liminf_{n\to\infty}\frac1n\mathbb E\log Z_n(a,\beta)\ge0.$$ For every fixed real $a>\alpha$, some positive integer $\beta$ satisfies $$\label{cp:soft-above} \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac1n\mathbb E\log Z_n(a,\beta)<0.$$
  - Explanation: Expected normalized log partition functions are asymptotically nonnegative below the threshold. Above it, the linear violation bound yields strict negativity for a suitable integer penalty.
- **claim-rational-trial-evaluation** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-ctr-evaluation)): For rational $a>0$, a positive integer $\beta$, and a finite description $Q\in\mathcal Q$, the value $G_1(a,\beta;Q)$ is uniformly computable from these data. In particular, strict negativity of this value is semidecidable by certified rational upper bounds.
  - Explanation: A valid rational trial has a uniformly computable value. Finite conditional sums, certified elementary functions, and a uniform Poisson tail bound semidecide strict negativity.
- **claim-pressure-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ctr-pressure)): For every $a>0$ and finite $\beta>0$, the sequence $L_n$ in Equation <a href="#ctr:pressure-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:pressure-data">[ctr:pressure-data]</a> is superadditive. Hence the finite limit $$P(a,\beta)=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{L_n}{n} =\sup_{n\ge1}\frac{L_n}{n} \label{ctr:pressure-limit}$$ exists and satisfies $\log2-\beta a\le P(a,\beta)\le\log2$.
  - Explanation: Sparse interpolation makes the expected log partition function superadditive. Linear bounds then yield a finite pressure limit equal to the supremum of the normalized finite-size values.
- **claim-sound-site-only-upper-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-ctr-upper)): For every $a>0$ and finite $\beta>0$, each finite-depth strict site-only trial satisfies $$L_n\le nG_1\quad(n\ge1),\qquad P(a,\beta)\le G_1. \label{ctr:upper-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Every strict finite-depth site-only trial bounds the finite-size pressure and its limit from above. Fresh reservoir occurrence independence and the nonnegative cubic remainder establish soundness.
- **claim-cascade-identities** (construction, [source](#stmt-ctr-rpc)): For $0<t_1<\cdots<t_d<1$, there are random positive weights $(v_\alpha)_{\alpha\in\mathbb N^d}$ with $\sum_\alpha v_\alpha=1$ and the following properties.* *Independent samples from these weights give nested exchangeable partitions by their common prefixes. Conditional on the partitions of $n\ge1$ sampled leaves, the probability that the next sample belongs to a specified already seen level-$j$ cluster $C$, for $1\le j\le d$, is $$\frac{n_C-t_j}{n}, \label{ctr:prediction}$$ where $n_C$ is the number of samples in $C$. These probabilities, and their differences between parent and child clusters, determine the partition law. The ranked cluster masses are the limiting sample frequencies.* *Take any root data independent of the cascade weights. Conditional on those data, attach independent uniform marks to the nonroot vertices, independently of the weights; any root marks are included in the root data. Each mark may be a vector of uniforms. Let $Y_\alpha$ be the value of the same bounded measurable function of the path marks and root data. Then $$\mathbb E\log\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb N^d}v_\alpha e^{Y_\alpha} =\mathbb ET_{t_1}\cdots T_{t_d}Y_{\mathrm{path}}. \label{ctr:rpc-log}$$ This identity holds conditionally on the root data. It also holds when $\|Y_\alpha\|\le B$ for all leaves, where $B$ depends only on the root data, is finite almost surely, and satisfies $\mathbb EB<\infty$.
  - Explanation: Strict exponents generate normalized cascade weights whose sample partitions obey explicit cluster predictions. Their mean logarithmic path average equals the nested conditional transform, including integrably root-bounded losses.
- **claim-finite-reservoir-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-ctr-reservoir)): Fix $a>0$ and finite $\beta>0$. For each positive integer $k$ and each $\epsilon>0$, there is a depth-one trial with $t_1=1$ such that $$\frac{G_k}{k}\le P(a,\beta)+\epsilon. \label{ctr:reservoir-bound}$$ Consequently there is a sequence of trials, one for each horizon $N\to\infty$, with $G_N/N\le P(a,\beta)+o(1)$. These trials may use a shared nonroot global variable, and the trial at one horizon need not be the trial at another.
  - Explanation: Finite reservoirs give nearly optimal total trial values at each fixed packet horizon. The initial trials can share a nonroot Gibbs assignment and need not coincide across horizons.
- **claim-path-posterior** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ctr-posterior)): Let $Y$ use a collection of old sites and let $X$ use fresh sites in one trial, with both losses bounded conditional on the root data. Define $$Y_\ell=T_{t_{\ell+1}}\cdots T_{t_d}Y\quad(0\le\ell\le d), \qquad Y_d=Y,\quad Y_0=\mathcal TY.$$ The difference $\mathcal T(Y+X)-\mathcal TY$ is the nested transform of $X$ when the successive conditional laws at level $\ell$ are changed by the density $$\exp\{t_\ell(Y_\ell-Y_{\ell-1})\}. \label{ctr:posterior-density}$$ The old site variables may be stored as additional global variables. In this representation all fresh site generators retain their independent uniform laws. Any property of the original fresh-site kernels and their conditional uniform site laws that holds for almost every complete global path survives this change of law.
  - Explanation: Subtracting an old-site transform produces an exact posterior hierarchy for fresh losses. The densities use old variables only, preserving fresh uniform site laws and almost-everywhere global-path records.
- **claim-depth-independent-sensitivity** (bound, [source](#stmt-cs-sensitivity)): Suppose $\|X\|\le C$ almost surely, where $C\ge0$. For any two nondecreasing vectors $\mathbf t,\mathbf t'\in(0,1]^d$, $$\label{cs:exponent-bound} \|\mathcal T_{\mathbf t}X-\mathcal T_{\mathbf t'}X\| \le K(C)\\|\mathbf t-\mathbf t'\\|_\infty, \qquad K(C)=\frac52 C^2e^{4C}.$$ For a single operation and $0\le t\le1$, $$\label{cs:small-exponent} 0\le T_tX-\mathbb EX\le\frac12 C^2e^{2C}t.$$ Both assertions hold conditionally on almost every root value. Their constants are independent of the depth and of the fixed sampling kernels.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous exponent changes incur a cost independent of hierarchy depth. A telescoping variance budget and bounded posterior densities control the aggregate derivative; a separate estimate controls the small-exponent limit.
- **claim-structural-transition** (construction, [source](#stmt-ct-transition)): Fix a density $a>0$, a finite penalty $\beta>0$, $\eta\in(0,1)$, $\delta>0$, and $m_0>0$. Suppose there is a sequence of active trials of fixed depth $d$ and fixed boundary indices, with horizons $N\to\infty$, satisfying $$\frac{G_N}{N}\le U+o(1),\qquad t_1\ge m_0, \label{ct:input-bound}$$ for a finite $U$. Suppose the old blocks satisfy Equations <a href="#ctr:band" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:band">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href="#ctr:record" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:record">[ctr:record]</a> with fixed finite continuous test families. Fix also a finite nonempty continuous $[0,1]$-valued test family on the type space of the current $S_1$. After passing to a subsequence along which all old exponents converge, for each fixed positive integer $k$ there is a finite-depth trial of horizon $k$ with $G_k/k\le U$ having the following properties.* *For some $h\in\{0,1,2\}$, the output has depth $d+h$: the $h$ new levels are inserted above the old first level, which becomes the *sample level* $h+1$. Every old block $(b,c]$ becomes $(b+h,c+h]$ and retains its exponent band, test family, tolerance, and record. One new processed block ends at level $h+1$ and satisfies Equations <a href="#ctr:band" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:band">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href="#ctr:record" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:record">[ctr:record]</a> for the specified new test family. Either this new block starts at the root, making the hierarchy fully processed, or it starts at the new first level and the hierarchy remains active, with first exponent $$0<m_{\mathrm{new}}\le(1-\eta)\lim t_1. \label{ct:progress}$$ All records hold for almost every complete global path.
  - Explanation: A selected limiting hierarchy preserves old records and adds a processed block at a fixed output horizon. Either processing reaches the root or the remaining first exponent contracts. The construction retains shared globals until the later collapse.
- **claim-conditional-gaussian-probing** (bound, [source](#stmt-ct-gaussian)): Let $\mu$ be a probability measure on a standard Borel space. Let $(C_l)_{l\ge1}$ be positive semidefinite measurable kernels with separable Hilbert-space feature representations and $0\le C_l(\zeta,\zeta)\le1$. Put $a_l=2^{-l}$ and let $g_l$ be independent centered Gaussian fields with covariances $C_l$. For $s\ge1$ and $x_l\in[1,2]$, set $$\label{ct:gaussian-tilt} h(\zeta)=s\sum_{l\ge1}a_lx_lg_l(\zeta) -\frac{s^2}{2}\sum_{l\ge1}a_l^2x_l^2 C_l(\zeta,\zeta), \qquad \mu^h(\,\mathrm d\zeta)=\frac{e^{h(\zeta)}\mu(\,\mathrm d\zeta)}{\mu(e^h)}.$$ The normalizer is finite and positive almost surely. Brackets denote conditionally independent samples from $\mu^h$ given the Gaussian fields, with any additional marks sampled by a kernel depending on those samples and with no further dependence on the fields. Define $$V_l(\zeta)=\frac{g_l(\zeta)}{s a_lx_l}-C_l(\zeta,\zeta), \qquad e_l=\mathbb E_g\left\langle \left\|V_l(\zeta^1)-\mathbb E_g\langle V_l(\zeta^1)\rangle\right\| \right\rangle.$$ Uniformly in $\mu$, in the kernels, and in the other parameters, $$\label{ct:error-integral} \int_1^2 e_l\,\,\mathrm dx_l=o_l(1) \qquad(s\longrightarrow\infty).$$ For every integer $n\ge1$ and every bounded measurable $f$, $\|f\|\le1$, of the first $n$ samples and their marks, $$\begin{aligned} \label{ct:approx-gg} \bigg\|n\mathbb E_g\langle fC_l(1,n+1)\rangle -\sum_{i=2}^n\mathbb E_g\langle fC_l(1,i)\rangle -\mathbb E_g\langle f\rangle\mathbb E_g\langle C_l(1,2)\rangle\bigg\| \le e_l. \end{aligned}$$ Here $C_l(i,j)=C_l(\zeta^i,\zeta^j)$.
  - Explanation: Compensated Gaussian tilts have finite positive normalizers and uniformly vanishing averaged probing error. Integration by parts yields marked approximate overlap identities for selected conditional laws.
- **claim-off-diagonal-ultrametricity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ct-gram-bridge)): Let $R=(R_{ij})_{i,j\ge1}$ be a symmetric, weakly exchangeable positive semidefinite array with $R_{ii}=1$. Suppose that for every $n\ge2$, every bounded Borel function $f$ of the off-diagonal entries among the first $n$ indices, and every bounded Borel function $\psi$, one has $$n\mathbb E[f\psi(R_{1,n+1})] =\mathbb E[f]\mathbb E[\psi(R_{12})]+\sum_{i=2}^n\mathbb E[f\psi(R_{1i})].$$ Then $R_{12}\ge\min\{R_{13},R_{23}\}$ almost surely, and the analogous inequality holds for every triple of distinct indices.
  - Explanation: Off-diagonal overlap identities imply ultrametricity for a weakly exchangeable positive semidefinite array with unit diagonal. The proof passes through a directing Hilbert-space law supported on a sphere before invoking the true Gram-array theorem.
- **claim-vector-overlap-ordering** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ct-ordering)): The support $\mathcal S$ of the off-diagonal law of $\mathbf Q$ in <a href="#ct:marked-gg" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ct:marked-gg">[ct:marked-gg]</a> is totally ordered componentwise. The scalar off-diagonal overlaps $$Q(u,v)=J_f^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{J_f}Q_j(u,v),\qquad u\ne v,$$ satisfy the ultrametric inequalities on triples of distinct indices, and, for $v,v'\in\mathcal S$, $$\label{ct:coordinate-control} \|v_j-v'_j\|\le J_f\left\|J_f^{-1}\sum_i(v_i-v'_i)\right\|.$$
  - Explanation: The vector-overlap support is componentwise ordered. Positive scalar combinations are ultrametric, and the scalar mean controls each coordinate's variation on that support.
- **claim-marked-cut-representation** (classification, [source](#stmt-ct-marked-cuts)): The nested cut partition has the sample-partition law of an RPC with parameters <a href="#ct:cut-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ct:cut-parameters">[ct:cut-parameters]</a>. The reindexed decorations $D'_{\alpha,l,\xi,i}$ are independent of the full partition and are hierarchically exchangeable in the hidden-cluster and sample coordinates. They retain the independent hierarchical exchangeabilities of the old descendant trees and the simultaneous exchangeability of the sites.
  - Explanation: Finite overlap cuts have the cascade partition law. Reindexed decorations are independent of the complete partition and preserve hidden-tree, descendant-tree, and site exchangeabilities.
- **claim-uniform-empirical-tree-evaluation** (bound, [source](#stmt-ct-empirical)): Let $C\ge0$, let $B\ge1$ be an integer, and let $\|X\|\le C$ in a hierarchy of fixed depth $d$ with exponents in $(0,1]$. At every vertex replace its conditional integral by the empirical average over $B$ conditionally independent children. The resulting nested logarithmic transform differs from the exact transform in mean absolute value by at most $$A(C,d)B^{-1/2},$$ uniformly in the conditional sampling kernels, the exponents, and the root data. The same statement holds for finitely many sites sampled jointly at every vertex.
  - Explanation: Replacing each conditional integral by independent empirical children approximates the nested transform in mean absolute value. The bound is uniform over the kernels and admissible exponents at fixed depth.
- **claim-endpoint-passage** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ct-endpoint)): Equip the represented hierarchy with exponents $$\label{ct:new-exponents} mx_1,\ldots,mx_h,\ m,\ t_2,\ldots,t_d.$$ For each endpoint of horizon $k$, its expected nested transform is the subsequential limit of the corresponding selected posterior endpoint. In particular its trial functional satisfies $G_k/k\le U$. The hidden list is omitted when $h=0$, and the old descendant list is omitted when $d=1$.
  - Explanation: Hidden cascade cuts contribute scaled exponents above the original sample level. Empirical evaluation, natural sample frequencies, and cascade logarithmic identities identify the limiting endpoint functional without changing its value bound.
- **claim-closed-hilbert-support-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-ct-support)): Let $V,V'$ be independent random variables with the same law in a separable Hilbert space, and let $\delta,\varepsilon\ge0$.* 1. *If $\|\langle V,V'\rangle\|\le\delta^2$ almost surely, then $\\|V\\|\le\delta$ almost surely.* 2. *If $\langle V,V'\rangle$ almost surely belongs to a fixed closed interval of length $\varepsilon$, then the support of their common law has diameter at most $\sqrt{2\varepsilon}$. Consequently $\\|V-\mathbb EV\\|\le\sqrt{2\varepsilon}$ almost surely whenever the expectation exists.* *If $V,V'$ are conditionally independent with the same conditional law given a standard Borel random element, the corresponding conclusions hold for almost every conditional law in which the stated pair bound holds.
  - Explanation: Closed pairwise inner-product constraints extend to the product of supports. They yield individual norm bounds or support diameter bounds, including after conditioning on a standard Borel random element.
- **claim-preserved-old-records** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ct-old-records)): Every old processed block satisfies Equation <a href="#ctr:record" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:record">[ctr:record]</a> in the represented hierarchy, with the same test family and tolerance and with its boundaries shifted by $h$.
  - Explanation: Old records survive the limiting representation with shifted boundaries. Closed empirical site constraints pass through weak convergence, and conditional support bounds restore the almost-everywhere global-path estimates.
- **claim-new-processed-record** (construction, [source](#stmt-ct-new-record)): Let $c=h+1$ be the sample level. Let $b=1$ if the parent cut is present, and let $b=0$ otherwise. There is an ancestor-measurable probability-valued type $S_b$ such that $(b,c]$ satisfies Equation <a href="#ctr:record" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ctr:record">[ctr:record]</a> for the specified new test family and tolerance $\delta$. Its exponents lie in $[m-\eta,m]$. If the parent cut is present, its exponent belongs to $(0,(1-\eta)m]$.
  - Explanation: A narrow overlap-support band yields an ancestor-measurable continuation type satisfying the new record. Its block fits the required exponent band, while a proper parent cut supplies contraction.
- **claim-finite-tests-for-site-laws** (bound, [source](#stmt-cc-site-law)): Given $e_0>0$ and a finite nonempty list of type spaces, one can choose finite nonempty collections $\Phi_j$ of $[0,1]$-valued $1$-Lipschitz functions such that, for probability measures $\nu,S$ on $K^{(j)}$, $$\label{cc:net-bound} W_1(\nu,S) \le\max_{\phi\in\Phi_j}\|\nu(\phi)-S(\phi)\|+e_0/2.$$ If the structural record holds with $$\label{cc:delta-choice} \bigl(\max_j\|\Phi_j\|\bigr)\delta\le e_0/2,$$ then, for each processed block $(b,c]$ and almost every fixed global path, $$\label{cc:site-law-distance} \int W_1\bigl(\nu_{z,u_{0:b}},S_b(z_{0:b},u_{0:b})\bigr) \,\,\mathrm du_{0:b}\le e_0.$$ Here $\nu_{z,u_{0:b}}$ is the conditional law of $S_c$ obtained by integrating only the fresh site coordinates $u_{b+1:c}$, with all the global coordinates fixed.
  - Explanation: Finite Lipschitz test families control Wasserstein distance on the compact type spaces. The mean-square structural records then give mean site-law distance bounds for almost every fixed global path.
- **claim-terminal-one-packet-trial** (construction, [source](#stmt-cc-terminal)): Fix $U>P(a,\beta)$, $0<\eta,m_0<1$, and an integer $J$ such that $$\label{cc:stopping-depth} (1-\eta)^J<m_0.$$ Choose in advance finite nonempty test sets on $K^{(j)}$, $0\le j\le J$, and a structural tolerance $\delta>0$. For every $\rho>0$ there is a trial with $$G_1\le U+\rho,$$ at most $J$ processed blocks, and depth at most $2J+1$, which satisfies the chosen structural records and has one of the following forms:* 1. *the processed blocks start at the root boundary $0$;* 2. *the processed blocks start at boundary $1$, and the remaining first exponent satisfies $0<m<m_0$.* *Every processed block has exponent width at most $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Repeated structural contraction reaches a terminal hierarchy within the chosen transition budget. A posterior packet telescope reduces it to a single packet while preserving records and terminal status.
- **claim-recorded-kernel-collapse** (construction, [source](#stmt-cc-kernel-collapse)): Consider a terminal trial from Proposition <a href="#cc:terminal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cc:terminal">28</a>, whose records imply Equation <a href="#cc:site-law-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cc:site-law-distance">[cc:site-law-distance]</a>. Fix a count cutoff $D_0\ge1$ and put $$C=\log2+\beta D_0,\qquad M=3D_0, \qquad R=JM e^\beta e^{2C(2J+2)}e_0.$$ There is a site-only trial, with weakly increasing exponents in $(0,1]$, such that $$\label{cc:collapse-error} \|G_1^{\mathrm{site}}-G_1\| \le 2K(C)\eta+2R+C^2e^{2C}m_0+2\tau_A+2\tau_B,$$ where $$\tau_A=\mathbb E[(\log2+\beta D_A)\mathbf 1_{\{D_A>D_0\}}],\qquad \tau_B=\mathbb E[(\log2+\beta D_B)\mathbf 1_{\{D_B>D_0\}}],$$ with $D_A\sim\operatorname{Poi}(3a)$ and $D_B\sim\operatorname{Poi}(2a)$. In the root-terminal case the term containing $m_0$ is unnecessary. The new message kernels are independent of the fresh packet counts and signs.
  - Explanation: Flattening recorded exponent bands permits independent site sampling from retained kernels. Wasserstein coupling bounds, depth-independent sensitivity, a possible small-exponent replacement, and Poisson tails control the change in trial value.
- **claim-finite-rational-completeness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cc-rational)): For every $a>0$ and finite $\beta>0$, $$\label{cc:finite-infimum} P(a,\beta)=\inf_{\mathcal Q}G_1,$$ where $\mathcal Q$ consists of site-only trials of finite depth whose exponents are strictly increasing rationals in $(0,1)$ and whose message pair is a rational-valued step function on a finite rational rectangular grid in its root and site uniform variables. In particular, this is a countable class with an effective enumeration.
  - Explanation: The pressure equals the infimum over effectively enumerable finite rational site-only trials. Non-effective limiting selections prove existence of witnesses; strict rational exponent approximation and finite message tables complete their finite encoding.
- **claim-enumerable-upper-certificates** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-assembly-upper)): For positive rational $a$, positive integer $\beta$, and a rational trial $Q$, the inequality $G_1(a,\beta;Q)<0$ certifies $\alpha\le a$. Every rational $a>\alpha$ has such a certificate. The class of certified densities is effectively enumerable.
  - Explanation: A negative rational trial value is a sound non-strict upper certificate for the threshold. Every strictly larger rational density has such a witness, and certified interval evaluation effectively enumerates the successful densities.

Hence the finite limit $$P(a,\\beta)=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}\\frac{L_n}{n}\n           =\\sup_{n\\ge1}\\frac{L_n}{n}\n \\label{ctr:pressure-limit}$$ exists and satisfies $\\log2-\\beta a\\le P(a,\\beta)\\le\\log2$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ctr-rpc",
        "exact_quote": "For $0<t_1<\\cdots<t_d<1$, there are random positive weights $(v_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}$ with $\\sum_\\alpha v_\\alpha=1$ and the following properties.*\n\n\n*Independent samples from these weights give nested exchangeable partitions by their common prefixes. Conditional on the partitions of $n\\ge1$ sampled leaves, the probability that the next sample belongs to a specified already seen level-$j$ cluster $C$, for $1\\le j\\le d$, is $$\\frac{n_C-t_j}{n},\n \\label{ctr:prediction}$$ where $n_C$ is the number of samples in $C$. These probabilities, and their differences between parent and child clusters, determine the partition law. The ranked cluster masses are the limiting sample frequencies.*\n\n\n*Take any root data independent of the cascade weights. Conditional on those data, attach independent uniform marks to the nonroot vertices, independently of the weights; any root marks are included in the root data. Each mark may be a vector of uniforms. Let $Y_\\alpha$ be the value of the same bounded measurable function of the path marks and root data. Then $$\\mathbb E\\log\\sum_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}v_\\alpha e^{Y_\\alpha}\n   =\\mathbb ET_{t_1}\\cdots T_{t_d}Y_{\\mathrm{path}}.\n \\label{ctr:rpc-log}$$ This identity holds conditionally on the root data. It also holds when $|Y_\\alpha|\\le B$ for all leaves, where $B$ depends only on the root data, is finite almost surely, and satisfies $\\mathbb EB<\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ctr-rpc",
          "exact_quote": "For $0<t_1<\\cdots<t_d<1$, there are random positive weights $(v_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}$ with $\\sum_\\alpha v_\\alpha=1$ and the following properties.*\n\n\n*Independent samples from these weights give nested exchangeable partitions by their common prefixes. Conditional on the partitions of $n\\ge1$ sampled leaves, the probability that the next sample belongs to a specified already seen level-$j$ cluster $C$, for $1\\le j\\le d$, is $$\\frac{n_C-t_j}{n},\n \\label{ctr:prediction}$$ where $n_C$ is the number of samples in $C$. These probabilities, and their differences between parent and child clusters, determine the partition law. The ranked cluster masses are the limiting sample frequencies.*\n\n\n*Take any root data independent of the cascade weights. Conditional on those data, attach independent uniform marks to the nonroot vertices, independently of the weights; any root marks are included in the root data. Each mark may be a vector of uniforms. Let $Y_\\alpha$ be the value of the same bounded measurable function of the path marks and root data. Then $$\\mathbb E\\log\\sum_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}v_\\alpha e^{Y_\\alpha}\n   =\\mathbb ET_{t_1}\\cdots T_{t_d}Y_{\\mathrm{path}}.\n \\label{ctr:rpc-log}$$ This identity holds conditionally on the root data. It also holds when $|Y_\\alpha|\\le B$ for all leaves, where $B$ depends only on the root data, is finite almost surely, and satisfies $\\mathbb EB<\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For $0<t_1<\\cdots<t_d<1$, there are random positive weights $(v_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}$ with $\\sum_\\alpha v_\\alpha=1$ and the following properties.*\n\n\n*Independent samples from these weights give nested exchangeable partitions by their common prefixes. Conditional on the partitions of $n\\ge1$ sampled leaves, the probability that the next sample belongs to a specified already seen level-$j$ cluster $C$, for $1\\le j\\le d$, is $$\\frac{n_C-t_j}{n},\n \\label{ctr:prediction}$$ where $n_C$ is the number of samples in $C$. These probabilities, and their differences between parent and child clusters, determine the partition law. The ranked cluster masses are the limiting sample frequencies.*\n\n\n*Take any root data independent of the cascade weights. Conditional on those data, attach independent uniform marks to the nonroot vertices, independently of the weights; any root marks are included in the root data. Each mark may be a vector of uniforms. Let $Y_\\alpha$ be the value of the same bounded measurable function of the path marks and root data. Then $$\\mathbb E\\log\\sum_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}v_\\alpha e^{Y_\\alpha}\n   =\\mathbb ET_{t_1}\\cdots T_{t_d}Y_{\\mathrm{path}}.\n \\label{ctr:rpc-log}$$ This identity holds conditionally on the root data. It also holds when $|Y_\\alpha|\\le B$ for all leaves, where $B$ depends only on the root data, is finite almost surely, and satisfies $\\mathbb EB<\\infty$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ctr-reservoir",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $a>0$ and finite $\\beta>0$. For each positive integer $k$ and each $\\epsilon>0$, there is a depth-one trial with $t_1=1$ such that $$\\frac{G_k}{k}\\le P(a,\\beta)+\\epsilon.\n \\label{ctr:reservoir-bound}$$ Consequently there is a sequence of trials, one for each horizon $N\\to\\infty$, with $G_N/N\\le P(a,\\beta)+o(1)$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ctr-posterior",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ use a collection of old sites and let $X$ use fresh sites in one trial, with both losses bounded conditional on the root data. Define $$Y_\\ell=T_{t_{\\ell+1}}\\cdots T_{t_d}Y\\quad(0\\le\\ell\\le d),\n \\qquad Y_d=Y,\\quad Y_0=\\mathcal TY.$$ The difference $\\mathcal T(Y+X)-\\mathcal TY$ is the nested transform of $X$ when the successive conditional laws at level $\\ell$ are changed by the density $$\\exp\\{t_\\ell(Y_\\ell-Y_{\\ell-1})\\}.\n \\label{ctr:posterior-density}$$ The old site variables may be stored as additional global variables. In this representation all fresh site generators retain their independent uniform laws. Any property of the original fresh-site kernels and their conditional uniform site laws that holds for almost every complete global path survives this change of law.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-cs-sensitivity",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $|X|\\le C$ almost surely, where $C\\ge0$. For any two nondecreasing vectors $\\mathbf t,\\mathbf t'\\in(0,1]^d$, $$\\label{cs:exponent-bound}\n |\\mathcal T_{\\mathbf t}X-\\mathcal T_{\\mathbf t'}X|\n \\le K(C)\\|\\mathbf t-\\mathbf t'\\|_\\infty,\n \\qquad K(C)=\\frac52 C^2e^{4C}.$$ For a single operation and $0\\le t\\le1$, $$\\label{cs:small-exponent}\n 0\\le T_tX-\\mathbb EX\\le\\frac12 C^2e^{2C}t.$$ Both assertions hold conditionally on almost every root value.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ct-transition",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a density $a>0$, a finite penalty $\\beta>0$, $\\eta\\in(0,1)$, $\\delta>0$, and $m_0>0$. Suppose there is a sequence of active trials of fixed depth $d$ and fixed boundary indices, with horizons $N\\to\\infty$, satisfying $$\\frac{G_N}{N}\\le U+o(1),\\qquad t_1\\ge m_0,\n \\label{ct:input-bound}$$ for a finite $U$. Suppose the old blocks satisfy Equations <a href=\"#ctr:band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:band\">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href=\"#ctr:record\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:record\">[ctr:record]</a> with fixed finite continuous test families. Fix also a finite nonempty continuous $[0,1]$-valued test family on the type space of the current $S_1$. After passing to a subsequence along which all old exponents converge, for each fixed positive integer $k$ there is a finite-depth trial of horizon $k$ with $G_k/k\\le U$ having the following properties.*\n\n*For some $h\\in\\{0,1,2\\}$, the output has depth $d+h$: the $h$ new levels are inserted above the old first level, which becomes the *sample level* $h+1$. Every old block $(b,c]$ becomes $(b+h,c+h]$ and retains its exponent band, test family, tolerance, and record. One new processed block ends at level $h+1$ and satisfies Equations <a href=\"#ctr:band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:band\">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href=\"#ctr:record\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:record\">[ctr:record]</a> for the specified new test family. Either this new block starts at the root, making the hierarchy fully processed, or it starts at the new first level and the hierarchy remains active, with first exponent $$0<m_{\\mathrm{new}}\\le(1-\\eta)\\lim t_1.\n \\label{ct:progress}$$ All records hold for almost every complete global path.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ct-transition",

      "exact_statement": "Fix a density $a>0$, a finite penalty $\\beta>0$, $\\eta\\in(0,1)$, $\\delta>0$, and $m_0>0$. Suppose there is a sequence of active trials of fixed depth $d$ and fixed boundary indices, with horizons $N\\to\\infty$, satisfying $$\\frac{G_N}{N}\\le U+o(1),\\qquad t_1\\ge m_0,\n \\label{ct:input-bound}$$ for a finite $U$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ct-gaussian",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a probability measure on a standard Borel space. Let $(C_l)_{l\\ge1}$ be positive semidefinite measurable kernels with separable Hilbert-space feature representations and $0\\le C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta)\\le1$. Put $a_l=2^{-l}$ and let $g_l$ be independent centered Gaussian fields with covariances $C_l$. For $s\\ge1$ and $x_l\\in[1,2]$, set $$\\label{ct:gaussian-tilt}\n h(\\zeta)=s\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_lx_lg_l(\\zeta)\n       -\\frac{s^2}{2}\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_l^2x_l^2 C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n \\mu^h(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)=\\frac{e^{h(\\zeta)}\\mu(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)}{\\mu(e^h)}.$$ The normalizer is finite and positive almost surely. Brackets denote conditionally independent samples from $\\mu^h$ given the Gaussian fields, with any additional marks sampled by a kernel depending on those samples and with no further dependence on the fields. Define $$V_l(\\zeta)=\\frac{g_l(\\zeta)}{s a_lx_l}-C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n e_l=\\mathbb E_g\\left\\langle\n  \\left|V_l(\\zeta^1)-\\mathbb E_g\\langle V_l(\\zeta^1)\\rangle\\right|\n \\right\\rangle.$$ Uniformly in $\\mu$, in the kernels, and in the other parameters, $$\\label{ct:error-integral}\n                         \\int_1^2 e_l\\,\\,\\mathrm dx_l=o_l(1)\n                         \\qquad(s\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ For every integer $n\\ge1$ and every bounded measurable $f$, $|f|\\le1$, of the first $n$ samples and their marks, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{ct:approx-gg}\n \\bigg|n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,n+1)\\rangle\n   -\\sum_{i=2}^n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,i)\\rangle\n   -\\mathbb E_g\\langle f\\rangle\\mathbb E_g\\langle C_l(1,2)\\rangle\\bigg|\n \\le e_l.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_l(i,j)=C_l(\\zeta^i,\\zeta^j)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.4"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ct-gaussian",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a probability measure on a standard Borel space. Let $(C_l)_{l\\ge1}$ be positive semidefinite measurable kernels with separable Hilbert-space feature representations and $0\\le C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta)\\le1$. Put $a_l=2^{-l}$ and let $g_l$ be independent centered Gaussian fields with covariances $C_l$. For $s\\ge1$ and $x_l\\in[1,2]$, set $$\\label{ct:gaussian-tilt}\n h(\\zeta)=s\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_lx_lg_l(\\zeta)\n       -\\frac{s^2}{2}\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_l^2x_l^2 C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n \\mu^h(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)=\\frac{e^{h(\\zeta)}\\mu(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)}{\\mu(e^h)}.$$ The normalizer is finite and positive almost surely. Brackets denote conditionally independent samples from $\\mu^h$ given the Gaussian fields, with any additional marks sampled by a kernel depending on those samples and with no further dependence on the fields. Define $$V_l(\\zeta)=\\frac{g_l(\\zeta)}{s a_lx_l}-C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n e_l=\\mathbb E_g\\left\\langle\n  \\left|V_l(\\zeta^1)-\\mathbb E_g\\langle V_l(\\zeta^1)\\rangle\\right|\n \\right\\rangle.$$ Uniformly in $\\mu$, in the kernels, and in the other parameters, $$\\label{ct:error-integral}\n                         \\int_1^2 e_l\\,\\,\\mathrm dx_l=o_l(1)\n                         \\qquad(s\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ For every integer $n\\ge1$ and every bounded measurable $f$, $|f|\\le1$, of the first $n$ samples and their marks, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{ct:approx-gg}\n \\bigg|n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,n+1)\\rangle\n   -\\sum_{i=2}^n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,i)\\rangle\n   -\\mathbb E_g\\langle f\\rangle\\mathbb E_g\\langle C_l(1,2)\\rangle\\bigg|\n \\le e_l.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_l(i,j)=C_l(\\zeta^i,\\zeta^j)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mu$ be a probability measure on a standard Borel space. Let $(C_l)_{l\\ge1}$ be positive semidefinite measurable kernels with separable Hilbert-space feature representations and $0\\le C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta)\\le1$. Put $a_l=2^{-l}$ and let $g_l$ be independent centered Gaussian fields with covariances $C_l$. For $s\\ge1$ and $x_l\\in[1,2]$, set $$\\label{ct:gaussian-tilt}\n h(\\zeta)=s\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_lx_lg_l(\\zeta)\n       -\\frac{s^2}{2}\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_l^2x_l^2 C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n \\mu^h(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)=\\frac{e^{h(\\zeta)}\\mu(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)}{\\mu(e^h)}.$$ The normalizer is finite and positive almost surely. Brackets denote conditionally independent samples from $\\mu^h$ given the Gaussian fields, with any additional marks sampled by a kernel depending on those samples and with no further dependence on the fields. Define $$V_l(\\zeta)=\\frac{g_l(\\zeta)}{s a_lx_l}-C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n e_l=\\mathbb E_g\\left\\langle\n  \\left|V_l(\\zeta^1)-\\mathbb E_g\\langle V_l(\\zeta^1)\\rangle\\right|\n \\right\\rangle.$$ Uniformly in $\\mu$, in the kernels, and in the other parameters, $$\\label{ct:error-integral}\n                         \\int_1^2 e_l\\,\\,\\mathrm dx_l=o_l(1)\n                         \\qquad(s\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ For every integer $n\\ge1$ and every bounded measurable $f$, $|f|\\le1$, of the first $n$ samples and their marks, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{ct:approx-gg}\n \\bigg|n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,n+1)\\rangle\n   -\\sum_{i=2}^n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,i)\\rangle\n   -\\mathbb E_g\\langle f\\rangle\\mathbb E_g\\langle C_l(1,2)\\rangle\\bigg|\n \\le e_l.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_l(i,j)=C_l(\\zeta^i,\\zeta^j)$.",
      "explanation": "Compensated Gaussian tilts have finite positive normalizers and uniformly vanishing averaged probing error. Integration by parts yields marked approximate overlap identities for selected conditional laws.",

        "anchor": "stmt-ct-gram-bridge",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R=(R_{ij})_{i,j\\ge1}$ be a symmetric, weakly exchangeable positive semidefinite array with $R_{ii}=1$.         "anchor": "stmt-ct-empirical",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C\\ge0$, let $B\\ge1$ be an integer, and let $|X|\\le C$ in a hierarchy of fixed depth $d$ with exponents in $(0,1]$.         "anchor": "stmt-ct-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V,V'$ be independent random variables with the same law in a separable Hilbert space, and let $\\delta,\\varepsilon\\ge0$.*\n\n1.  *If $|\\langle V,V'\\rangle|\\le\\delta^2$ almost surely, then $\\|V\\|\\le\\delta$ almost surely.*\n\n2.  *If $\\langle V,V'\\rangle$ almost surely belongs to a fixed closed interval of length $\\varepsilon$, then the support of their common law has diameter at most $\\sqrt{2\\varepsilon}$. Consequently $\\|V-\\mathbb EV\\|\\le\\sqrt{2\\varepsilon}$ almost surely whenever the expectation exists.*\n\n*If $V,V'$ are conditionally independent with the same conditional law given a standard Borel random element, the corresponding conclusions hold for almost every conditional law in which the stated pair bound holds.",
        "source_id": "PM-235.4"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Proper random clause and random formula** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $n\ge3$, a proper random 3-clause chooses three distinct variables uniformly from $x_1,\ldots,x_n$ and gives each variable an independent fair sign. Let $\Phi(n,m)$ be the conjunction of $m\ge0$ independent clauses with this law. Thus its clauses are sampled with replacement from the $8\binom n3$ possibilities; the formula with no clauses is satisfiable.
- **Satisfiability probability** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $$p(n,m)=\mathbb P\{\Phi(n,m)\text{ is satisfiable}\}.$$
- **Clause density** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The ratio $m/n$ is the clause density.
- **Relaxed Poisson model** ([source](#cp:section)): For every integer $n\ge1$, clauses arrive in a Poisson process of rate one. Each of their three variable indices is sampled independently and uniformly from $[n]$, and each sign is sampled independently and fairly. All indices, signs, and the clock are independent. Repetitions *within* a clause are permitted; time $an$ therefore gives a Poisson number of these clauses with mean $an$. We call this the *relaxed Poisson model*.
- **Deletion satisfiability** ([source](#cp:section)): For an integer $r$ with $0\le r\le n$, a formula is called $r$-deletion satisfiable if some set of at most $r$ variables can be deleted so that the remaining formula is satisfiable; deleting a variable discards every clause that touches it.
- **Capped deletion stopping time** ([source](#cp:section)): Let $T_r$ be the first time $r$-deletion satisfiability fails, with $T_r=+\infty$ if it never fails, and put $$F_r=T_r\wedge Bn.$$
- **Free deletion of a specified variable** ([source](#cp:section)): For a fixed variable $v$, a superscript $+v$ means that $v$ is deleted for free and up to $r$ *other* variables may be deleted.
- **Mean capped first-unsatisfiable time and survival** ([source](#cp:section)): Write $$f_n=\mathbb EF_0,\qquad P_n(t)=\mathbb P(T_0>t).$$
- **Soft partition function and expected logarithm** ([source](#ctr:pressure-data)): If $H_n(\sigma)$ is the number of clauses violated by $\sigma\in\{0,1\}^n$, put $$Z_n=\sum_{\sigma\in\{0,1\}^n}e^{-\beta H_n(\sigma)}, \qquad L_n=\mathbb E\log Z_n,\qquad w=1-e^{-\beta}. \label{ctr:pressure-data}$$
- **Strict hierarchy of averaging exponents** ([source](#ctr:exponents)): A trial is called *strict* when $0<t_1<\cdots<t_d<1$.
- **Sign-indexed messages** ([source](#pm-h-14)): The same two measurable functions at every site give the messages $$f^\pm(z_0,z_1,\ldots,z_d;u_0^v,u_1^v,\ldots,u_d^v)\in[0,1]. \label{ctr:messages}$$
- **Nested logarithmic transform** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For a loss depending on finitely many sites, define $$\mathcal TX=T_{t_1}\cdots T_{t_d}X. \label{ctr:transform}$$
- **A-packet loss** ([source](#ctr:A)): Its loss is $$X_A=\log\sum_{b=0,1}\prod_{h=1}^{D} \bigl(1-wI_h(b)f_{h1}f_{h2}\bigr). \label{ctr:A}$$
- **B-packet loss** ([source](#ctr:B)): Its loss is $$X_B=\sum_{h=1}^{D}\log\bigl(1-wf_{h1}f_{h2}f_{h3}\bigr). \label{ctr:B}$$
- **Total packet functional** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For a positive integer $k$, use fresh sites and independent clause data in each of $k$ packets and define the *total* value $$G_k=\mathbb E\mathcal T\sum_{i=1}^kX_{A,i} -\mathbb E\mathcal T\sum_{i=1}^kX_{B,i}. \label{ctr:G}$$
- **Site-only trial** ([source](#ctr:additive)): A trial is *site-only* if its message functions do not use $z_1,\ldots,z_d$.
- **Site-only packet additivity** ([source](#ctr:additive)): G_k=kG_1\qquad\text{for every site-only trial.} \label{ctr:additive}$$
- **Rational trial class** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A *rational trial* is a strict site-only trial in which $z_0$ is uniform on $[0,1]$, all exponents are rational, and the message pair is a rational-valued step function on a finite rational rectangular grid in $$(z_0,u_0,u_1,\ldots,u_d)\in[0,1]^{d+2}.$$
- **Iterated site type spaces** ([source](#pm-h-20)): Let $K^{(0)}=[0,1]^2$ with the sup metric and, recursively, let $$K^{(j+1)}=\mathcal P(K^{(j)})$$ with the Wasserstein–$1$ metric induced by the preceding metric.
- **Pathwise structural record** ([source](#ctr:record)): For a processed block $(b,c]$ with $S_c\in K^{(j)}$, the required record is $$\int\left\| \int\phi(S_c)\,\,\mathrm du_{b+1:c}-S_b(\phi) \right\|^2\,\mathrm du_{0:b}\le\delta^2 \qquad(\phi\in\Phi_j), \label{ctr:record}$$ for almost every fixed complete global path, including its root.

Repetitions *within* a clause are permitted; time $an$ therefore gives a Poisson number of these clauses with mean $an$. We call this the *relaxed Poisson model*.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }
      ],

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-threshold-precision | \|q_r-\alpha_3\| | ≤ | $2^{-r}$ | Unary precision supplied to the fixed deterministic machine. |
| q-time-cap | B | = | $20$ | Fixed truncation constant. |
| q-deletion-moment-exponent | p | = | $\frac65$ | Fixed moment exponent. |
| q-concentration-dual-exponent | b | = | $\frac56$ | Reciprocal of the fixed moment exponent. |
| q-free-deletion-moment-bound | \mathbb E\bigl[(F_r^{+v}-F_r)^p\bigr] | ≤ | $2^{60}$ | Uniform across sizes, deletion allowances, and distinguished variables. |
| q-capped-time-concentration | \mathbb E\lvert F_r-\mathbb EF_r\rvert^p | ≤ | $C_1n$ | All admissible capped deletion allowances. |
| q-concentration-constant | C_1 | = | $2^{62}$ | Fixed explicit constant. |
| q-one-sided-threshold-certificate | \alpha | ≥ | $\frac{f_k}{k}-2^{67}k^{-1/6}$ | Every finite formula size gives a lower certificate. |
| q-threshold-lower-location | \alpha | ≥ | $1/200$ | Limiting density identified by strict-side probability limits. |
| q-threshold-upper-location | \alpha | ≤ | $10$ | Limiting density identified by strict-side probability limits. |
| q-deletion-robustness-constant | C_4 | = | $2^{53}$ | Uniform deletion sensitivity constant. |
| q-pressure-lower-bound | P(a,\beta) | ≥ | $\log2-\beta a$ | Positive density and finite positive penalty. |
| q-pressure-upper-bound | P(a,\beta) | ≤ | $\log2$ | Positive density and finite positive penalty. |
| q-trial-pressure-upper-bound | P(a,\beta) | ≤ | $G_1$ | Strict finite-depth site-only trials with shared root data allowed. |
| q-rational-trial-poisson-tail | \mathbb E\bigl[(\log2+\beta D)\mathbf 1_{\{D>M\}}\bigr] | ≤ | $2^{-M}e^{\lambda}(\log2+2\beta\lambda)$ | The respective A-packet and B-packet mean counts. |
| q-depth-independent-sensitivity-coefficient | K(C) | = | $\frac52 C^2e^{4C}$ | Fixed sampling kernels and bounded losses; independent of hierarchy depth. |
| q-small-exponent-gap | T_tX-\mathbb EX | ≤ | $\frac12 C^2e^{2C}t$ | Almost every conditional root law. |
| q-structural-contraction | m_{\mathrm{new}} | ≤ | $(1-\eta)\lim t_1$ | The contraction applies when the new processed block does not start at the root. |
| q-empirical-tree-approximation | Mean absolute empirical tree transform error | ≤ | $A(C,d)B^{-1/2}$ | Uniform in kernels, admissible exponents, and root data at fixed depth. |
| q-terminal-hierarchy-depth | Terminal trial depth | ≤ | $2J+1$ | The stopping budget is chosen before the structural iteration. |
| q-recorded-kernel-collapse-error | \|G_1^{\mathrm{site}}-G_1\| | ≤ | $2K(C)\eta+2R+C^2e^{2C}m_0+2\tau_A+2\tau_B,$ | Both packet endpoints; the small-exponent term can be omitted in the root-terminal case. |

We suppress the dependence on $n$ in these times. For this fixed size, the overview’s $T_n$ is $T_0$ here; both denote the uncapped first-unsatisfiable time, whereas $F_0=T_0\\wedge Bn$ is capped. Write $$f_n=\\mathbb EF_0,\\qquad P_n(t)=\\mathbb P(T_0>t).$$ Thus $f_n$ is the mean first-unsatisfiable time capped at $20n$.",
            "source_id": "PM-235.4"
          }
        ],

          "anchor": "cp:constants",
          "exact_quote": "$$\\label{cp:constants}\n B=20,\\qquad p=\\frac65,\\qquad b=\\frac1p=\\frac56.$$ For an integer $r$ with $0\\le r\\le n$, a formula is called $r$-deletion satisfiable if some set of at most $r$ variables can be deleted so that the remaining formula is satisfiable; deleting a variable discards every clause that touches it. Let $T_r$ be the first time $r$-deletion satisfiability fails, with $T_r=+\\infty$ if it never fails, and put $$F_r=T_r\\wedge Bn.$$ The formula at time $t$ includes all arrivals up to and including $t$. Accordingly it survives at time $t$ exactly when $T_r>t$. For a fixed variable $v$, a superscript $+v$ means that $v$ is deleted for free and up to $r$ *other* variables may be deleted. In particular, $F_r^{+v}=T_r^{+v}\\wedge Bn\\ge F_r$, with the same cap $Bn$. Allowing more deletions than there are other variables has its literal meaning; it simply permits deleting all of them.         {
          "anchor": "stmt-ct-transition",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a density $a>0$, a finite penalty $\\beta>0$, $\\eta\\in(0,1)$, $\\delta>0$, and $m_0>0$. Suppose there is a sequence of active trials of fixed depth $d$ and fixed boundary indices, with horizons $N\\to\\infty$, satisfying $$\\frac{G_N}{N}\\le U+o(1),\\qquad t_1\\ge m_0,\n \\label{ct:input-bound}$$ for a finite $U$. Suppose the old blocks satisfy Equations <a href=\"#ctr:band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:band\">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href=\"#ctr:record\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:record\">[ctr:record]</a> with fixed finite continuous test families. Fix also a finite nonempty continuous $[0,1]$-valued test family on the type space of the current $S_1$. After passing to a subsequence along which all old exponents converge, for each fixed positive integer $k$ there is a finite-depth trial of horizon $k$ with $G_k/k\\le U$ having the following properties.*\n\n*For some $h\\in\\{0,1,2\\}$, the output has depth $d+h$: the $h$ new levels are inserted above the old first level, which becomes the *sample level* $h+1$. Every old block $(b,c]$ becomes $(b+h,c+h]$ and retains its exponent band, test family, tolerance, and record. One new processed block ends at level $h+1$ and satisfies Equations <a href=\"#ctr:band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:band\">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href=\"#ctr:record\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:record\">[ctr:record]</a> for the specified new test family. Either this new block starts at the root, making the hierarchy fully processed, or it starts at the new first level and the hierarchy remains active, with first exponent $$0<m_{\\mathrm{new}}\\le(1-\\eta)\\lim t_1.\n \\label{ct:progress}$$ All records hold for almost every complete global path.",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-certificate-search** [claim](#stmt-cert-search) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cert-search): Fair finite dovetailing of lower certificates and certified negative upper witnesses. -> A certified shrinking interval and a terminating rational midpoint approximation.
- **step-deletion-moment** [claim](#stmt-cp-deletion-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-deletion-moment): Residual-set conditioning, replacement of shorter tests, dyadic survival bins, and martingale bucket resampling. -> Uniform free-deletion delay moments and capped-time concentration.
- **step-weighted-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-cp-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-interpolation): Poisson interpolation followed by pairwise variable-weight averaging through parity-character expansions. -> A product lower bound on union survival probability.
- **step-limiting-center** [claim](#stmt-cp-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-lower): Concentration converts survival interpolation into almost-superadditivity; dyadic merges make normalized errors summable. -> Convergence of normalized capped means and explicit finite-size lower certificates.
- **step-threshold-transfer** [claim](#stmt-cp-threshold) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-threshold): First-moment and matching bounds, concentration around the limiting mean, thinning, and Poisson count comparisons with density slack. -> Strict-side threshold limits for both relaxed and proper ensembles.
- **step-finite-size-computation** [claim](#stmt-cp-finite-computation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-finite-computation): Exhaustive finite clause and assignment enumeration, survival integration, explicit Poisson tails, and certified exponential intervals. -> Uniformly computable capped expectations and convergent strict rational lower certificates.
- **step-robust-violations** [claim](#stmt-cp-robust) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-robust): Sum marginal deletion gains using the uniform moment bound, then apply concentration and remove variables from violated clauses. -> Robust deletion failure and a positive density of unavoidable violations.
- **step-soft-signs** [claim](#stmt-cp-soft-signs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cp-soft-signs): Bound the log partition function above and below using the minimum violation count. -> Nonnegative lower pressure signs below the threshold and negative upper signs above it.
- **step-trial-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-ctr-evaluation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ctr-evaluation): Finite rational grid sums, certified elementary functions, and packet count-tail truncation. -> Uniform certified intervals for the trial value and a semidecision of strict negativity.
- **step-pressure-limit** [claim](#stmt-ctr-pressure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ctr-pressure): Sparse Poisson interpolation and convexity of signed-literal proportions yield superadditivity. -> A finite normalized pressure limit with explicit linear bounds.
- **step-cascade-identities** [claim](#stmt-ctr-rpc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ctr-rpc): Marked Poisson mapping, stable-sum moments, factorial-moment partition predictions, and backward path reweighting. -> Normalized cascade weights, nested partition predictions, and the logarithmic transform identity.
- **step-site-only-upper-bound** [claim](#stmt-ctr-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ctr-rpc-2): Cascade interpolation between real clauses and fresh reservoir occurrences, with a nonnegative cubic remainder. -> Sound finite-size and limiting pressure upper bounds.
- **step-finite-reservoir** [claim](#stmt-ctr-reservoir) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ctr-reservoir): Couple old/new variable formula increments to ideal packet rates and select large reservoirs along a favorable increment subsequence. -> Nearly optimal depth-one trials at each fixed horizon, initially sharing nonroot data.
- **step-path-posterior** [claim](#stmt-ctr-posterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ctr-posterior): Subtract successive conditional log transforms and normalize their exponential densities. -> An exact posterior trial preserving fresh uniform site generators and pathwise records.
- **step-exponent-sensitivity** [claim](#stmt-cs-sensitivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cs-sensitivity): A telescoping conditional variance budget and ordered-parameter density bounds control the aggregate derivative. -> Depth-independent exponent sensitivity and a small-exponent replacement bound.
- **step-gaussian-probing** [claim](#stmt-ct-gaussian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-gaussian): Compensated Gaussian tilting, convex normalizer derivatives, concentration, and integration by parts. -> Uniformly vanishing averaged errors and marked approximate overlap identities.
- **step-gram-ultrametricity** [claim](#stmt-ct-gram-bridge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-gram-bridge): Directing-measure representation, sphere support, and ultrametricity of the true Gram array. -> Ultrametric inequalities on distinct triples.
- **step-overlap-ordering** [claim](#stmt-ct-ordering) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-ordering): Apply scalar ultrametricity to positive rational combinations and exclude incomparable support neighborhoods through overlap identities. -> Componentwise ordering and scalar control of coordinate differences.
- **step-marked-cuts** [claim](#stmt-ct-marked-cuts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-marked-cuts): Conditional cut predictions identify the cascade partition and prove decoration independence after partition-measurable reindexing. -> Partition-independent hierarchically exchangeable decorations.
- **step-empirical-tree** [claim](#stmt-ct-empirical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-empirical): Bound one-step empirical exponential errors and propagate mean errors recursively through the finite hierarchy. -> Uniform empirical tree transform approximation.
- **step-endpoint-limit** [claim](#stmt-ct-endpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-endpoint): Pass continuous empirical evaluations through weak limits, recover exact transforms, and convert natural cluster frequencies through cascade logarithmic identities. -> Represented endpoint values with the scaled hidden exponent list.
- **step-support-bounds** [claim](#stmt-ct-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-support): Extend closed full-product-measure pair constraints to the product of supports and apply Hilbert-space identities. -> Individual norm and support diameter bounds, also conditionally.
- **step-old-records** [claim](#stmt-ct-old-records) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-old-records): Pass finite continuous site-column constraints through weak convergence and identify conditional error-vector inner products by site averages. -> Preservation of old structural records at shifted boundaries.
- **step-new-record** [claim](#stmt-ct-new-record) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-new-record): Use a narrow ordered-overlap band, empirical site products, and conditional support diameters to define an ancestor continuation type. -> A new processed block with its pathwise record, exponent band, and possible first-exponent contraction.
- **step-structural-transition** [claim](#stmt-ct-transition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ct-new-record-2): Select perturbed posterior laws, pass to compact array limits, represent decorations, and assemble endpoint and record conclusions. -> A fixed-horizon hierarchy with another recorded block and either root completion or contraction.
- **step-finite-site-tests** [claim](#stmt-cc-site-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-site-law): Finite uniform nets of Lipschitz tests and Kantorovich–Rubinstein duality turn structural records into site-law distance estimates. -> Pathwise mean Wasserstein control of continuation laws.
- **step-terminal-hierarchy** [claim](#stmt-cc-terminal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-terminal): Iterate structural contraction with fixed tests and then telescope packets through an old-data posterior. -> A terminal one-packet hierarchy with bounded depth and retained records.
- **step-kernel-collapse** [claim](#stmt-cc-kernel-collapse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-kernel-collapse): Flatten recorded exponent bands, couple site-law kernels, propagate Lipschitz errors, and move a residual small-exponent global into root data. -> A site-only trial with an explicit collapse error bound.
- **step-rational-completeness** [claim](#stmt-cc-rational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cc-rational): Choose approximation tolerances in advance, collapse shared variables, strictify exponents, and approximate measurable messages by finite rational grids. -> Equality of pressure with the infimum over effectively enumerable rational trials.
- **step-upper-certificates** [claim](#stmt-assembly-upper) / [proof](#proof-stmt-assembly-upper): Combine pressure signs, sound upper bounds, rational completeness, and certified negativity evaluation. -> Effectively enumerable upper certificates at every strictly larger rational density.
- **step-main-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-assembly-upper-2): Identify the proper-clause threshold and instantiate the bilateral certificate search with the proved lower and upper families. -> A fixed deterministic threshold approximation machine driven only by unary precision.

Hence the finite limit $$P(a,\\beta)=\\lim_{n\\to\\infty}\\frac{L_n}{n}\n           =\\sup_{n\\ge1}\\frac{L_n}{n}\n \\label{ctr:pressure-limit}$$ exists and satisfies $\\log2-\\beta a\\le P(a,\\beta)\\le\\log2$.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ctr-rpc",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ctr-rpc",
          "exact_quote": "For $0<t_1<\\cdots<t_d<1$, there are random positive weights $(v_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}$ with $\\sum_\\alpha v_\\alpha=1$ and the following properties.*\n\n\n*Independent samples from these weights give nested exchangeable partitions by their common prefixes. Conditional on the partitions of $n\\ge1$ sampled leaves, the probability that the next sample belongs to a specified already seen level-$j$ cluster $C$, for $1\\le j\\le d$, is $$\\frac{n_C-t_j}{n},\n \\label{ctr:prediction}$$ where $n_C$ is the number of samples in $C$. These probabilities, and their differences between parent and child clusters, determine the partition law. The ranked cluster masses are the limiting sample frequencies.*\n\n\n*Take any root data independent of the cascade weights. Conditional on those data, attach independent uniform marks to the nonroot vertices, independently of the weights; any root marks are included in the root data. Each mark may be a vector of uniforms. Let $Y_\\alpha$ be the value of the same bounded measurable function of the path marks and root data. Then $$\\mathbb E\\log\\sum_{\\alpha\\in\\mathbb N^d}v_\\alpha e^{Y_\\alpha}\n   =\\mathbb ET_{t_1}\\cdots T_{t_d}Y_{\\mathrm{path}}.\n \\label{ctr:rpc-log}$$ This identity holds conditionally on the root data. It also holds when $|Y_\\alpha|\\le B$ for all leaves, where $B$ depends only on the root data, is finite almost surely, and satisfies $\\mathbb EB<\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-cascade-identities",
      "inputs": [
        "Strict hierarchy exponents",
        "Independent hierarchical marks",
        "Integrable root-conditional loss bounds"

      "output": "Normalized cascade weights, nested partition predictions, and the logarithmic transform identity.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ctr-rpc"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-ctr-posterior",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ use a collection of old sites and let $X$ use fresh sites in one trial, with both losses bounded conditional on the root data. Define $$Y_\\ell=T_{t_{\\ell+1}}\\cdots T_{t_d}Y\\quad(0\\le\\ell\\le d),\n \\qquad Y_d=Y,\\quad Y_0=\\mathcal TY.$$ The difference $\\mathcal T(Y+X)-\\mathcal TY$ is the nested transform of $X$ when the successive conditional laws at level $\\ell$ are changed by the density $$\\exp\\{t_\\ell(Y_\\ell-Y_{\\ell-1})\\}.\n \\label{ctr:posterior-density}$$ The old site variables may be stored as additional global variables. In this representation all fresh site generators retain their independent uniform laws. Any property of the original fresh-site kernels and their conditional uniform site laws that holds for almost every complete global path survives this change of law.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cs-sensitivity",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cs-sensitivity",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $|X|\\le C$ almost surely, where $C\\ge0$. For any two nondecreasing vectors $\\mathbf t,\\mathbf t'\\in(0,1]^d$, $$\\label{cs:exponent-bound}\n |\\mathcal T_{\\mathbf t}X-\\mathcal T_{\\mathbf t'}X|\n \\le K(C)\\|\\mathbf t-\\mathbf t'\\|_\\infty,\n \\qquad K(C)=\\frac52 C^2e^{4C}.$$ For a single operation and $0\\le t\\le1$, $$\\label{cs:small-exponent}\n 0\\le T_tX-\\mathbb EX\\le\\frac12 C^2e^{2C}t.$$ Both assertions hold conditionally on almost every root value. Their constants are independent of the depth and of the fixed sampling kernels.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ct-gaussian",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ct-gaussian",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be a probability measure on a standard Borel space. Let $(C_l)_{l\\ge1}$ be positive semidefinite measurable kernels with separable Hilbert-space feature representations and $0\\le C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta)\\le1$. Put $a_l=2^{-l}$ and let $g_l$ be independent centered Gaussian fields with covariances $C_l$. For $s\\ge1$ and $x_l\\in[1,2]$, set $$\\label{ct:gaussian-tilt}\n h(\\zeta)=s\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_lx_lg_l(\\zeta)\n       -\\frac{s^2}{2}\\sum_{l\\ge1}a_l^2x_l^2 C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n \\mu^h(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)=\\frac{e^{h(\\zeta)}\\mu(\\,\\mathrm d\\zeta)}{\\mu(e^h)}.$$ The normalizer is finite and positive almost surely. Brackets denote conditionally independent samples from $\\mu^h$ given the Gaussian fields, with any additional marks sampled by a kernel depending on those samples and with no further dependence on the fields. Define $$V_l(\\zeta)=\\frac{g_l(\\zeta)}{s a_lx_l}-C_l(\\zeta,\\zeta),\n \\qquad\n e_l=\\mathbb E_g\\left\\langle\n  \\left|V_l(\\zeta^1)-\\mathbb E_g\\langle V_l(\\zeta^1)\\rangle\\right|\n \\right\\rangle.$$ Uniformly in $\\mu$, in the kernels, and in the other parameters, $$\\label{ct:error-integral}\n                         \\int_1^2 e_l\\,\\,\\mathrm dx_l=o_l(1)\n                         \\qquad(s\\longrightarrow\\infty).$$ For every integer $n\\ge1$ and every bounded measurable $f$, $|f|\\le1$, of the first $n$ samples and their marks, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{ct:approx-gg}\n \\bigg|n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,n+1)\\rangle\n   -\\sum_{i=2}^n\\mathbb E_g\\langle fC_l(1,i)\\rangle\n   -\\mathbb E_g\\langle f\\rangle\\mathbb E_g\\langle C_l(1,2)\\rangle\\bigg|\n \\le e_l.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $C_l(i,j)=C_l(\\zeta^i,\\zeta^j)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-gaussian-probing",
      "inputs": [
        "Separable positive semidefinite feature kernels",
        "Fixed conditional sampling law",
        "Field-independent additional marks"
      ],
      "method": "Compensated Gaussian tilting, convex normalizer derivatives, concentration, and integration by parts.",
      "output": "Uniformly vanishing averaged errors and marked approximate overlap identities.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-ct-gaussian"
    },

Suppose that for every $n\\ge2$, every bounded Borel function $f$ of the off-diagonal entries among the first $n$ indices, and every bounded Borel function $\\psi$, one has $$n\\mathbb E[f\\psi(R_{1,n+1})]\n =\\mathbb E[f]\\mathbb E[\\psi(R_{12})]+\\sum_{i=2}^n\\mathbb E[f\\psi(R_{1i})].$$ Then $R_{12}\\ge\\min\\{R_{13},R_{23}\\}$ almost surely, and the analogous inequality holds for every triple of distinct indices.",

The scalar off-diagonal overlaps $$Q(u,v)=J_f^{-1}\\sum_{j=1}^{J_f}Q_j(u,v),\\qquad u\\ne v,$$ satisfy the ultrametric inequalities on triples of distinct indices, and, for $v,v'\\in\\mathcal S$, $$\\label{ct:coordinate-control}\n             |v_j-v'_j|\\le J_f\\left|J_f^{-1}\\sum_i(v_i-v'_i)\\right|.$$",

          "exact_quote": "Equip the represented hierarchy with exponents $$\\label{ct:new-exponents}\n                  mx_1,\\ldots,mx_h,\\ m,\\ t_2,\\ldots,t_d.$$ For each endpoint of horizon $k$, its expected nested transform is the subsequential limit of the corresponding selected posterior endpoint.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-ct-support",
      "depends_on": [],

          "anchor": "stmt-ct-support",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V,V'$ be independent random variables with the same law in a separable Hilbert space, and let $\\delta,\\varepsilon\\ge0$.*\n\n1.  *If $|\\langle V,V'\\rangle|\\le\\delta^2$ almost surely, then $\\|V\\|\\le\\delta$ almost surely.*\n\n2.  *If $\\langle V,V'\\rangle$ almost surely belongs to a fixed closed interval of length $\\varepsilon$, then the support of their common law has diameter at most $\\sqrt{2\\varepsilon}$. Consequently $\\|V-\\mathbb EV\\|\\le\\sqrt{2\\varepsilon}$ almost surely whenever the expectation exists.*\n\n*If $V,V'$ are conditionally independent with the same conditional law given a standard Borel random element, the corresponding conclusions hold for almost every conditional law in which the stated pair bound holds.",

        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-ct-transition",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ct-transition",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a density $a>0$, a finite penalty $\\beta>0$, $\\eta\\in(0,1)$, $\\delta>0$, and $m_0>0$. Suppose there is a sequence of active trials of fixed depth $d$ and fixed boundary indices, with horizons $N\\to\\infty$, satisfying $$\\frac{G_N}{N}\\le U+o(1),\\qquad t_1\\ge m_0,\n \\label{ct:input-bound}$$ for a finite $U$. Suppose the old blocks satisfy Equations <a href=\"#ctr:band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:band\">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href=\"#ctr:record\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:record\">[ctr:record]</a> with fixed finite continuous test families. Fix also a finite nonempty continuous $[0,1]$-valued test family on the type space of the current $S_1$. After passing to a subsequence along which all old exponents converge, for each fixed positive integer $k$ there is a finite-depth trial of horizon $k$ with $G_k/k\\le U$ having the following properties.*\n\n*For some $h\\in\\{0,1,2\\}$, the output has depth $d+h$: the $h$ new levels are inserted above the old first level, which becomes the *sample level* $h+1$. Every old block $(b,c]$ becomes $(b+h,c+h]$ and retains its exponent band, test family, tolerance, and record. One new processed block ends at level $h+1$ and satisfies Equations <a href=\"#ctr:band\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:band\">[ctr:band]</a> and <a href=\"#ctr:record\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ctr:record\">[ctr:record]</a> for the specified new test family. Either this new block starts at the root, making the hierarchy fully processed, or it starts at the new first level and the hierarchy remains active, with first exponent $$0<m_{\\mathrm{new}}\\le(1-\\eta)\\lim t_1.\n \\label{ct:progress}$$ All records hold for almost every complete global path.",
          "source_id": "PM-235.4"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-structural-transition",
      "inputs": [
        "Active growing-horizon trials",
        "Fixed boundary pattern and tests",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-exact-finite-satisfiability**: The satisfiability probability with a fixed finite number of relaxed clauses is rational and can be computed by exhaustive enumeration of signed clause lists and assignments.
- **invariant-relaxed-clause-distribution**: Repeated variable positions within a clause are permitted; positions and signs are sampled independently.
- **invariant-empty-formula-satisfiable**: The formula with no clauses is satisfiable.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-enumeration-as-threshold-certificate**: Treat exact agreement on finite formula probabilities as a certificate of the limiting threshold or of the complete computability proof. -> Finite enumeration checks a subroutine; it does not establish convergence, the lower correction, or completeness of the upper certificate family.
- **anti-relaxed-as-proper-finite-probability**: Report the enumerated relaxed-model probability as the proper-clause probability at the same finite size. -> The enumeration permits repeated variable positions, whereas proper clauses require distinct variables.
- **anti-instance-solver**: Use the threshold approximation as a satisfying-assignment search for an input formula. -> The approximation returns a scalar limiting density rather than an assignment for a supplied instance.
- **anti-efficiency-from-halting**: Infer an efficient practical computation from the deterministic halting guarantee. -> The existence of a terminating search supplies no efficiency estimate.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The checked construction is the finite rational satisfiability probability subroutine in the proof of effective finite-size integration. It exhaustively enumerates relaxed signed clause lists and assignments. This bounded check does not certify the limiting threshold, the infinite Poisson integral, or the non-effective selections establishing rational trial completeness.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer n and m.
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= n <= 3; 0 <= m <= 2, or n == 1 and m == 3.
# OUTPUTS: JSON numerator and denominator of the exact relaxed-model satisfiability probability.
# TIME: Exhaustive enumeration of (8*n**3)**m clause lists and 2**n assignments.
# SPACE: Stores the finite clause alphabet, assignment truth table, and current clause list.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and sympy Rational; no floating-point arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import itertools
import json
import sys
from sympy import Rational

data = json.load(sys.stdin)
if type(data) is not dict or set(data) != {'n', 'm'}:
    raise ValueError('expected n and m')
n, m = data['n'], data['m']
if type(n) is not int or type(m) is not int:
    raise ValueError('integer parameters required')
if not (1 <= n <= 3 and (0 <= m <= 2 or (n == 1 and m == 3))):
    raise ValueError('outside checked domain')
clauses = [tuple(zip(indices, signs))
           for indices in itertools.product(range(n), repeat=3)
           for signs in itertools.product((0, 1), repeat=3)]
assignments = list(itertools.product((0, 1), repeat=n))
def clause_true(assignment, clause):
    return any(bit == sign for variable, sign in clause for bit in (assignment[variable],))
truth = [[clause_true(assignment, clause) for clause in clauses]
         for assignment in assignments]
clause_count = len(clauses)
satisfiable = 0
for formula in itertools.product(range(clause_count), repeat=m):
    if any(all(row[c] for c in formula) for row in truth):
        satisfiable += 1
probability = Rational(satisfiable, clause_count ** m)
print(json.dumps({'numerator': int(probability.p), 'denominator': int(probability.q)}, sort_keys=True))
```
